# Consolidated reading: changes against witnesses C and H

Counts refer to distinct documented substantive loci, not tokens. A locus may differ from both witnesses. Pure macro substitutions and editorial witness headings are excluded. Rows with neither witness-change flag set remain fully published below as noncounted observations, but are excluded from locus, witness, confidence and feedback-category totals. Certainty-only observations remain in the doubt apparatus and may also be retained as explicitly noncounted rows.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 003, upper-right oblique marginal note (s003-03): Restores the factor l₁^{-γ} in the conjugating element g₀.
- Scan 003, last display, small superscript on rho (s003-16): A live inverse on ρ differs from a cancelled inverse; the corrected relation cannot be settled from the expected algebra.
- Scan 003, last display, inverse on the value of rho (s003-23): The inverse is written after the inner closing parenthesis. Its position is preserved even though an automorphism commutes with taking an inverse.
- Scan 003, parameter paragraph, group containing g-one (s003-24): The written group sign is capital Pi in this clause; the small pi of the opening identification is not globally replaced.
- Scan 005, two-line title (s005-02): The omitted title identifies the profinite loop group and its (0,3) index.
- Scan 005, Un endomorphisme de … (s005-05): The indexed group is read consistently with the explicit capital-Pi title definition, while the small unindexed prose pi remains lowercase.
- Scan 005, interlinear insertion below the last sigma formula (s005-09): The deletion-layer reading remains provisional; the live inner-conjugation operand is retained as l_0^{-1}.
- Scan 007, class-normalizing phrase between two main prose lines (s007-06): The phrase specifies the exterior automorphism whose representative is being chosen.
- Scan 007, parenthetical description of L_0 (s007-08): The manuscript uses an isomorphism sign, not an equality sign.
- Scan 007, added character value above p in the multiplier sentence (s007-10): The addition identifies p with chi(u); it attaches before the membership statement.
- Scan 007, opening sentence, indexed group on which tau operates (s007-14): Capital Pi follows the written indexed group definition; unindexed pi later on this page stays lowercase.
- Scan 008, opening line, object of remplace (s008-02): Preserves a probable authorial g/u slip instead of silently changing the object of remplace.
- Scan 008, first display, argument of rho (s008-03): The expected argument is lambda; the ink now remains l0/lambda rather than being declared a certain authorial slip.
- Scan 008, live replacement above far-right end of third system row (s008-08): Preserves the author’s unmatched parenthesis in the replacement expression.
- Scan 008, last live display, first int expression (s008-10): The unmatched closing parenthesis is preserved literally.
- Scan 008, last live display, int-rho expression before target l-infinity (s008-11): No balancing parenthesis is silently supplied.
- Scan 009, Annotations beneath u sigma_infty(l_0) (s009-07): Preserves the separate equality annotation and the scope of the lower bracket.
- Scan 009, Left underbrace in the second calculation (s009-09): Attaches l_0 to sigma_infty(l_1), excluding the preceding u.
- Scan 010, Marginal equations between abélien and donc (s010-07): The note asserts equality of beta and gamma and gives q=-r as its equivalent condition; it does not state the two separate identifications written by H.
- Scan 010, Last marginal formula, exponent on the final l_1 (s010-08): Both generator factors in the last marginal expression carry beta.
- Scan 010, Small insertion above que in the final paragraph (s010-17): The phrase supplies the additional condition p=±1 at the point en exigeant ... que.
- Scan 011, Group definition: name after equality (s011-16): The author names the group Γ.
- Scan 011, Continuation: gamma inserted after une autre (s011-18): The symbol γ labels the second function, with values in the quotient.
- Scan 011, Application display: domain (s011-19): The displayed application has domain Γ.
- Scan 011, Oblique margin: heading and map (s011-21): The marginal map is γ₀, not an unindexed γ.
- Scan 012, First display inside the large cancelled paragraph (s012-01): The written left factor may be g rather than the rho expected from the name of the relation; no mathematical correction is silently made.
- Scan 012, Cancelled line beginning à L_0 (s012-02): Retains capital subgroup notation in the cancelled argument.
- Scan 012, Second cancelled display (s012-03): The capital L forms are secure; the argument index remains unresolved.
- Scan 012, Cancelled line after et ceci détermine (s012-04): Restores the stated modulo qualification without resolving the nested cancelled expression.
- Scan 012, Cancelled line multiplication à droite (s012-05): The manuscript speaks of an element of L_1; puissance is not present.
- Scan 012, Last line of the cancelled paragraph, before donnée (s012-06): Preserves the authorial wording le donnée; the obscured preposition remains uncertain.
- Scan 012, Article and inserted prefix before extension (s012-09): The insertion belongs to extension, after the article; neither a corrected article sequence nor a grammatical normalization is imposed.
- Scan 012, Interlinear insertion after par π (s012-10): Restores the locally written Aut_lac and the attachment of its ambient-group qualification.
- Scan 012, First live sigma-rho identity (s012-11): Restores the inverse exponent in the written relation.

- Scan 019, long product calculation, two annotation brackets (s019-19): The labels retain their exact factor attachments; only the shape of the written annotation rules is changed.
- Scan 020, Left margin, l_infinity square and initial-rho exponent state (s020-10): A cancelled inverse exponent gives the loop word (rho sigma0)^2 verified in the local C2*C3 calculation; a live inverse gives a different word. The ink state remains uncertain, so both are retained.
- Scan 020, Introduction to (7), sigma relation (s020-22): The high mark has no minus; its distinction as prime versus numeral1 remains unresolved. The inverse dotted rho belongs to the lower index.
- Scan 020, System (8), third-row final bare l-infinity; apparent exponent reassigned to the second-row replacement (s020-36): The apparent final inverse was the separate replacement l1 beside the preceding row; it supplies no exponent to this row.
- Scan 020, system(8), global cancellation scope and second-row replacement (s020-40): The explicitly inserted l1 is associated with the second row. The overall abandonment of the proposal may be intended, but exact deletion scope is not inferred from the next page.
- Scan 021, NB margin, index on the general right-hand sigma (s021-04): Preserves the dotted permutation acting on the index and its literal notation.
- Scan 021, First main paragraph, comparison with Gamma (s021-10): The written relation is inclusion, not equality.
- Scan 021, (15), infinity row, sign between the two products (s021-36): The written relation sign is equivalence. The local word checks still test the equality of the two words and cannot choose between the drawn sign shapes.
- Scan 022, Three-line question following (10) (s022-08): The literal que non conflicts with the first surviving product, which solves for rho as a word in the epsilons. Both are preserved; the algebraic rearrangement is given in the notes and check events.
- Scan 024, Relation (21), complete formula and dark exponent state (s024-17): The live inverse and cancelled inverse give different conventions for conjugating elements; this first reader made no algebraic choice between them.
- Scan 025, First preliminary formula, both g_i exponents (s025-05): The exponent state is not decided from either conjugation convention.
- Scan 025, (24), surviving upper-right insertion and parenthesis count (s025-18): The written outer parenthesis remains unmatched; no corrected cocycle formula is substituted.
- Scan 025, Final kernel map, domain immediately before arrow (s025-32): The domain is transcribed without the exclamation that is visible on the preceding group and on the map target; no mathematical completion is supplied.
- Scan 026, covariance formula, final argument (s026-17): The two u adornments are preserved as distinct; no rho action is supplied on the right.
- Scan 026, Opening alpha family, small mark beside alpha (s026-32): The local small mark is preserved at its apparent height; the outer family index remains i in{0,1,infinity}.
- Scan 027, Opening extension: delimiters around the three indices of the ornate S (s027-01): Preserves the written subscript delimiters without replacing them by conventional set braces.
- Scan 027, Fundamental-group display: all three initial Pi letters (s027-02): Preserves the capital group notation Π, Π0,3 and Π1.
- Scan 027, Fundamental-group display: base point after the last comma (s027-03): Restores the written base point -bar j.
- Scan 027, Fundamental-group display: right delimiter after the base point (s027-04): Leaves the authorial mismatch of opening parenthesis and closing brace visible.
- Scan 027, Invariant list: cancelled sign before the first tau (s027-07): Whole-prefix cancellation remains preferred, making tau the first live member; a surviving rho with only its high2 cancelled is explicitly retained as a state alternative. No fixed-point calculation selects between those ink states.
- Scan 027, Les pts fixes de rho: absence of over-dot on the live rho (s027-08): Preserves the undotted rho of this occurrence.
- Scan 027, Stabilizer list after formé de: three sigma indices (s027-12): Retains the written indices 1,2,3 despite the 0,1,infinity convention used elsewhere on this same sheet.
- Scan 027, Parenthetical Pi-conjugués: the group letter (s027-14): The capital Pi of the conjugacy qualification is resolved from its two full posts and cap.

### Remaining unresolved places

- opening, word between commutant and sigma-zero: \deleted{\unsure{à}} — Dense crossed knot; neither the underlying letter nor cancellation state can be fully distinguished.
- first display, argument of u: u(l_{\unsure{1|0}}) — The index is an opaque teardrop of ink. The target l₁^p suggests 1, but that is not a recovery of the individual strokes.
- long left marginal note: \unsure{\beta=\gamma|\beta\ne\gamma} — The comparison sign is oblique and crowded by adjacent letters.
- rewritten second relation, crossed exponent before beta: \deleted{\unsure{\gamma|\alpha}} — The crossed loop has no fully recoverable outline; omitted by both witnesses.
- last display, underlying first argument of int: \deleted{\unsure{h_1}} — Additional strokes under the live h₁′ are present, but every letter is not plain.
- last display, cancelled factor before l-infinity: \deleted{\unsure{l_1^\mu|h_1}} — The clear closing curve after h1-prime stands to the left of the raised minus1, followed by the outer int closing curve. Both first witnesses had put this inverse inside the inner parenthesis. The cancelled earlier factor has intersecting tall strokes admitting l1-to-mu or h1. The gamma cluster admits an unprimed gamma as well as the previous alternatives. Above rho the black roof can be a cancelled inverse or a hat; neither the old glyph nor its state is secure. The saved full-formula crop confirms the same placement.
- last display, exponent of l-infinity: \unsure{\gamma'|\gamma-\mu|\gamma} — The clear closing curve after h1-prime stands to the left of the raised minus1, followed by the outer int closing curve. Both first witnesses had put this inverse inside the inner parenthesis. The cancelled earlier factor has intersecting tall strokes admitting l1-to-mu or h1. The gamma cluster admits an unprimed gamma as well as the previous alternatives. Above rho the black roof can be a cancelled inverse or a hat; neither the old glyph nor its state is secure. The saved full-formula crop confirms the same placement.
- last display, rho superscript: \unsure{\rho^{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}}|\rho^{-1}|\rho^1|\hat\rho} — The clear closing curve after h1-prime stands to the left of the raised minus1, followed by the outer int closing curve. Both first witnesses had put this inverse inside the inner parenthesis. The cancelled earlier factor has intersecting tall strokes admitting l1-to-mu or h1. The gamma cluster admits an unprimed gamma as well as the previous alternatives. Above rho the black roof can be a cancelled inverse or a hat; neither the old glyph nor its state is secure. The saved full-formula crop confirms the same placement.
- parenthetical gamma-prime equality, cancelled term: \deleted{\unsure{+2\mu|+\mu}} — The possible 2 cannot be separated from the diagonal cancellation.
- closing beta-prime equality, underlying symbol: \deleted{\unsure{\beta}} — The live prime is clear, but the earlier complete β outline is not recoverable.
- closing prose before (2)_2: \unsure{à|:} — The lower body is compact rather than a fully separable a-loop; upper and lower marks also permit punctuation.
- boxed relation (2), mark before h-one: \unsure{\cdot|=} — One dark compact horizontal mark lies between l-infinity to minus-gamma and h1. A paler short parallel trace lies underneath. The final equals sign has two dark strokes; here the lower trace cannot securely be assigned to writing rather than paper. The whole relation was reread.
- definition of h-one, upper mark on sigma: \unsure{\sigma_0|\sigma_0'|\dot\sigma_0} — A tiny dark tick or point occurs at the upper right of sigma beyond its roof. It could be a prime, a point, or the end of its entry stroke; the crop does not securely distinguish them. The lower0 and the remaining inverse-product definition agree.
- boxed relation (1-prime), first inner-automorphism argument boundary: \unsure{\operatorname{int}(h_1)|\operatorname{int}h_1} — At the junction after int, the terminal t stroke, opening curve and h stem touch. The closing curve after h1 and the following l1 argument are present. The crop does not fully separate an opening argument parenthesis from the neighbouring letter stroke.

### Remaining unresolved places

- struck glyph in the title before profini: \deleted{\unsure{P|$\Pi$}} — The glyph is heavily retraced and crossed. Not all pre-cancellation strokes can be separated, and the boundary from the initial of profini may warrant review; retain the deletion doubt rather than resolving a struck letter from context.
- isolated left-margin sign: $\unsure{\partial|\delta}$ — The isolated closed oval with hooked oblique stem admits both signs; no local definition settles its function.
- struck operand inside the inserted int expression: \deleted{\unsure{l_0}} — Several dark strokes overlap the live operand. They do not yield a fully plain struck l and subscript 0. The deletion hypothesis is retained from C but requires root adjudication of the ink layering.
- first inserted symmetric-group name: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The symbol is a rounded S with a hooked upper-right stroke and closed lower loop; this page alone does not define its typographic alphabet.
- final symmetric-group formula: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3^+|\mathcal S_3^+} — Same unresolved alphabet distinction as the inserted group name. The superscript plus and the isomorphism sign are plain.
- Oblique extension-group index: \mathcal E_{\unsure{0,3|03}} — A separate final curved s follows the n of extensions. Three independent underline strokes follow the three slanted baselines. The0 and3 are plain, but their joining comma cannot be separated securely from the thick3 entry; retain0,3 first with03 as an alternative.
- Un endomorphisme, preposition before group: \unsure{de|du} — The upper cap and returning second leg of the retraced group sign are consistent with the defined capital Pi. The small d has a closed bowl and upright, but its trailing low loop does not securely distinguish e from u. The same physical locus is covered in B16.
- On définit de barred-m, second sigma index: \sigma_{\unsure{1|\infty}} — The overbar is detached from a low curved m. The second sigma index has a dark upright and an adjoining mass;1 remains preferred but infinity cannot be excluded. The first l after et merges its foot with a small loop, allowing infinity or0; the later infinity and final0 are more distinct. The long guide attaches the parenthesis after the second sigma.
- Exchange insertion, first loop after et: l_{\unsure{\infty|0}} — The overbar is detached from a low curved m. The second sigma index has a dark upright and an adjoining mass;1 remains preferred but infinity cannot be excluded. The first l after et merges its foot with a small loop, allowing infinity or0; the later infinity and final0 are more distinct. The long guide attaches the parenthesis after the second sigma.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening prose after pas de: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- Live dans at the right of the cancelled display: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- On a une représentation de: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- vu que ... opérant: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate handwritten S is clear, but the isolated page does not settle the LaTeX alphabet. This is an alphabet-classification uncertainty, not doubt about S or the index 3.
- Cancelled first display, middle product: \unsure{\sigma_0^{-1}\sigma_1^{-1}} — Cancellation hatching prevents every base letter/index from being verified; C’s proposed product remains possible.
- sur pi lui-bar-m insertion: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — Thickened two-legged handwritten pi; case is not secure in the small inserted form.
- Main line after Z/3Z and its insertion: \unsure{provient|pvt} — The form has an unmistakable p-descender and final tall t but strongly compressed interior; a contraction pvt is possible. Both witnesses give provient. This is a newly marked certainty question, not an asserted expansion.
- Fixed-point insertion before the explanatory parenthesis: \unsure{\pm\bar j|-\bar j|\pm j} — Reinforcing strokes obscure the initial sign; the long horizontal above j may be a bar, although a very elongated dot is not wholly excluded.
- Cancelled word following qu’on: \deleted{\unsure{pourra}} — The cancellation covers the interior of the word; not all letters are plain.
- Short live form after the cancelled word, before next-line pris: \unsure{a} — The short form is probably a but has an unusually long initial descending stroke; preserved with a new doubt.
- Right of first calculation: \margin{$\unsure{2|z|t|\tau}$} — A dark upper hooked/crossed cap descends diagonally to a lower rightward foot. It remains compatible with2 or a letter-like trace; tau is a reasonable additional reading, not a resolution. Its placement away from the equation is preserved.
- cancelled terminal factor in the third calculation: \deleted{\unsure{l_1|l_1^2|l_0^2}} — The second sigma0-to-l0 junction has a faint curve crowded against the l loop; the opening parenthesis is plausible but not independently plain. The cancelled terminal l retains a dark lower index and an upper hooked trace that may be a2 or part of the hatching. Retain l1 first, while allowing l1 squared and l0 squared. All other displayed factors and signs agree; aligned/display wrappers do not alter the reading.
- Qualification after du moins: \unsure{pas sur|pour|par sur} — After the clear du moins, a p stem and pale low strokes are followed by a separated dark s-like rise and a u/r continuation. The second reading exposes a two-word possibility that the first compressed to pour. The pale middle letters do not justify choosing pas against par absolutely or excluding the earlier one-word reading.
- Second calculation, sigma0 argument opening: \sigma_0\unsure{(}l_0)^{-1} — The left curve is crowded into the tall l loop; the second reading led to retaining a local presence doubt.

### Remaining unresolved places

- first symmetric-group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The rounded S-like form does not by itself settle the typographic alphabet; retain M0780.
- symmetric-group symbol in the product defining Gamma_0,3: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Same local graphic form as the earlier symbol; the whole product formula was read in its crop, but the alphabet remains unresolved.
- crossed word before L_0: \deleted{\unsure{un}} — The heavy strike obscures the original low letter strokes. The reading un remains plausible but is not plain letter for letter.
- small upper-line stroke following the inserted =chi(u): \inserted{$=\chi(u)$,} — Both witnesses read a comma. A comma-like stroke is visible just above the right endpoint of the curved insertion guide. Their near contact leaves a genuine placement/status question for root review; the proposed reading retains the comma within the upper-line addition.
- interlinear normalizing clause, final object: \unsure{l_0|tout} — The main line has a separate accented à followed by the loop and long descending stem of rho, then a stop and Notons que. Below, tout has the tall initial t, and autom repeats the same short form, followed by extérieur and d’un autom. In the interlinear note, normalise has a tall l and small i dot, followed by la classe de conj. The far-right cluster can be read as l0, but its tall final turn also permits the blind reading tout; that local uncertainty is retained rather than resolved by mathematical sense.
- multiplier sentence, cancellation scope in est: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{est}} est|est|\deleted{\unsure{es}}t} — The parameter has an upright stem and small right bowl matching p in the following display. The equality and chi(u) sit above the line, with a curved guide after p and a separate comma-like stroke above its endpoint. After hatted Z-star, a heavy horizontal cluster overlaps the e/s region while a tall final t survives; the crop does not securely distinguish a replaced whole est from a partial cancellation within one est.
- relation (**), old product immediately after equals: \deleted{\unsure{\rho u}} — The old rho loop and descending stem are visible; the succeeding low u course is crossed and merged with the cancellation. Not every stroke of the old two-symbol product is plain. The live int(g) rho u and terminal comma remain distinct.

### Remaining unresolved places

- word cancelled before live les relations: \deleted{\unsure{les|la}} — The old initial tall loop survives, but its low terminal group is heavily crossed and filled. It does not separate es from a securely. The following live les relations is clear.
- third system row, label below the rho-conjugation product: \unsure{\operatorname{int}(\rho(g))} — The small closing curves in the underbrace label are crowded; there may be only one closing parenthesis after g.
- cancelled end of third system row: \deleted{\unsure{\operatorname{int}(g\rho(g))l_\infty^p|\operatorname{int}g\rho(g)l_\infty^p|\operatorname{int}(g\rho(g))l_\infty}} — In the live upper-right replacement, int runs directly into g with no separate opening curve; two closing curves follow g-rho(g). The lower annotation’s closing curves remain crowded. The old term is crossed and reaches the physical right edge, where a final p cannot be isolated securely. The second reading’s balanced live expression supplies a curve not visible in the ink.
- oblique NB note, subject after que: \unsure{$u$|$g$|$u_g$} — The upper form is a dark u-like pair of turns, and a lower loop with descender is separately visible. It could be a subscript g or an unresolved g/u overwrite; no cancellation is unambiguous. Automatiquement includes the long final ment course. A small accented body precedes lacets, and a separate comma follows it. The page already has the margin wrapper.
- closing prose, sign immediately after (*): \unsure{(*)2|(*)_2|(*),} — Small terminal turns support Les and relations. The second reference has two dark star clusters; the first has a separate low2-like character after its closing curve. It is low enough to permit a subscript but not securely detached below the baseline, and a retraced comma remains possible. The new doubt spans the entire reference so that both placement readings are represented.
- first display, argument of rho: \unsure{l_0|\lambda} — The tall curved stroke is followed by a small rounded lower body, supporting l0. The blind lambda reading is also permitted because the lower body touches the descending leg and could be its forked terminal rather than a separate index. The initial lambda is more clearly forked. The distinction is no longer considered certain; the underline and inverse placement agree.
- parameter paragraph, group after g-in: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — The upper line ends with termes, and the next begins directly with p. The group sign after g has a narrow cap and merged vertical strokes; pi remains preferred but a capital Pi cannot be excluded. The unit mark retains the local starred convention.

### Remaining unresolved places

- rho i.e. S_3^+: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3^+|\mathcal S_3^+} — The ornate S is secure, as are 3 and +; typographic alphabet remains unsettled.
- mais à S_3 tout entier: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Same local alphabet uncertainty; no uncertainty about the index 3.
- NB margin, after opère: \unsure{triv\textsuperscript{t}} — Small interlocked abbreviation with raised terminal; the proposed expansion is not substituted for its written form.
- Cancelled factor after int(h): \deleted{\unsure{u|\tau}} — The old character is blackened by a roof-like crossing stroke. A low u-like curve survives, but a tau stem under the same stroke is possible. The surrounding int(h), live sigma-infinity and terminal u are distinct; no extra live factor is introduced.
- Beginning of int argument in cancelled bottom equation: \unsure{gl_1^q} — There is a small entangled trace between the first g and l_1^q under the cancellation; the retained grouped reading is provisional.
- after mod autom extérieurs: \unsure{à|:} — The word has a small initial e, crossed x, tall t, a dotted i and the low eurs course; it is not relation. The following sign has an upper short nearly vertical stroke and a dark lower body. That body permits a compressed a, but its loop is not cleanly separated from a dot, so a colon remains possible. The preposition is preferred without being declared certain.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Cancelled passage after et pas du: \deleted{\unsure{choix de u|choix de ce}} — The strike crosses choix and both following groups. The ch ascender, dotted i and part of de survive, but the last low turns can be read as u or ce under the crossing stroke. The live de son représentant is separate. Normalisateur is divided across a manuscript line; continuous lexical transcription was already documented.
- tau calculation, equals between l0 and l0-to-r: \unsure{=|=:} — The connective has the d loop, low on and short final c, without the further ne course of donne. Two small dark points stand just above/right of the equals bars. Their high position permits incidental pen points, but the adjacent colon-like pair cannot be dismissed securely; equals remains the preferred reading. The q=-1 and r=1 values and final stop are clear.
- end of the small donc p=plus-minus1 insertion: \unsure{et|et)} — The inserted parameter and the two raised exponents share the defined p with a straight descending stem and upper bowl. The small word after1 has an e turn and tall t, without the two dotted i letters required for ici. A separate thin curved stroke lies just to its right; it may be a closing parenthesis or an insertion-related stroke. The leader before que still establishes attachment of the insertion.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{pour $g$}} — The g descender is clear; several strokes of pour remain crossed and faint.
- Opening insertion, symbol after savoir: \unsure{\tau|\zeta} — The small symbol combines a short upper stroke with a hooked or zigzag continuation. Tau remains the first reading, but the local shape alone leaves a zeta-like alternative.
- Opening abbreviation: \unsure{correspond.|correspond.\textsuperscript{t}} — The stop is visible; a small raised adjoining trace is not securely separable from the nearby cancelled writing.
- Opening cancelled word before à: \deleted{\unsure{aux}} — Three low letter traces support aux, but the strike prevents certainty for every letter.
- NB cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{deux}} — Initial d and terminal crossing are visible; the central e/u traces remain overlapped.
- NB function word ending: \unsure{fonction|fonctions} — The angular terminal tail can be a final n flourish or an additional s. The singular is retained first without grammatical normalization.
- Group definition, ornate group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ink establishes the ornate S with index 3, but not an exact typographic alphabet.
- Cancelled word following group definition: \deleted{\unsure{donnée|savoir}} — The initial rounded stroke and low internal courses are crossed; donnée remains possible, but the final dotted/looped cluster also allows savoir. The small accented a-like form is visibly above the crossed word. The whole-page layout was consulted for this attachment: the following valeurs begins the next line without another à, so this raised sign supplies that preposition.
- Oblique margin relation: \unsure{=|\simeq} — The compact uneven strokes do not settle equality versus the proposed isomorphism sign.
- Oblique margin cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{groupe|fixant}} — A thin curved leader under the raised gamma0-to-E0-to-pi map returns toward est un, establishing insertion. The numeral1 is followed by a small low point, not a horizontal hyphen. A separate opening square bracket stands at the left of the cancelled word, with no closing bracket visible. The old initial loop and descender allow groupe or fixant but not a complete certain recovery. The raised replacement has q/u/i followed by d/f ascenders, dotted i strokes and final t: qui définit. The subsequent le ss-gr. des éléments qui commutent ext. à is readable; the final ornate S has lower3. The relation after E0 remains uncertain, and the pi signs are read with the page’s explicit pi definition.
- Oblique margin final group symbol: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornate S is legible, while the exact font family remains uncertain.
- Closing cancellation: \deleted{\unsure{et|$\beta$}} — Inside the dense oblique crossing strokes, both an e/t sequence and a two-lobed beta outline remain possible. The live word défini ends at its final dotted i, without a separate e. The final pi has a rightward hooked foot. A distinct closing curve follows the hatted-Z argument and the outer mod expression; the blind excerpt omitted the latter. These clear live letters are not normalized by agreement.
- head numeral: \unsure{4|9} — An angular upper triangle meets a nearly horizontal bar and a long hooked right stem. That structure favors4, but the filled upper junction could also close the bowl of an angular9. The enlargement does not justify certainty between them; the page sequence is not used to decide.
- label above definition equality: \unsure{\mathrm{dfn}|\mathrm{def}} — The upper pi has a hooked right foot, and two close nearly parallel strokes below it form the vertical equality; they do not spread as a vee. The hatted indexed pi has curved feet. The small equality label has a rounded initial d, a tall descending middle stroke and a dark low final shoulder. That last shoulder favors n after f, while a compressed e/f reading remains possible. No independent accent is visible. The ornate S retains its alphabet doubt and the group name has the short right tip of Gamma.
- after inserted gamma, before valeurs: \unsure{à|:} — Gamma stands above the gap after autre. Immediately after it, two small dark bodies form the compact sign before valeurs; the lower body does not expose a secure a loop. The wording favors the a-with-accent reading, but the ink also permits a colon, so both are preserved.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Cancelled relation, first product: u\unsure{g|\rho} — The head and hooked descender favour g; the cancelled strokes do not justify discarding rho. Root independently agreed with g first.
- Cancelled image formula, last index: L_{\unsure{1|0}} — The final index is densely blackened.
- Nested cancellation after déjà: \deleted{\unsure{$\operatorname{int}(g)$}} — The strike overlaps several of the proposed small letters.
- Last line of cancelled paragraph, first word: \unsure{par|près|puis} — The initial p-like descender is clear, but overwriting obscures the middle. A small upper point and final turn also permit puis. The following article has a tall l and open e loop, not the closed a of la; the grammatical slip le donnée remains. No final stop follows u.
- Article before inserted sous-: \unsure{la|l'} — An a-shaped baseline body and an apostrophe-like upper stroke coexist. The correction sequence cannot be established.
- Ornamental S_3 in extension phrase: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The ornamental letter does not securely distinguish modern font choices in this local instance.
- Cancelled abbreviation after first sigma-rho identity: \deleted{\unsure{p.ex.}} — The strike crosses the letters and stops after p.
- Cancelled noun below autom.: \deleted{\unsure{él\textsuperscript{t}}} — Individual upper and baseline strokes overlap the cancellation and replacement.
- Cancelled terminal baseline word/sign: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{de|$\sigma$}}|$\sigma$} — After dans the low article body is a, followed by c, a tall l, and connected a/s/s/e, then m/o/d. The group sign has a capped upright body with a hooked inner/right leg and does not securely distinguish pi from capital Pi. The et after sigma-i is definitely crossed. At the far right, by contrast, the long upper stroke can be the cap of a live sigma-shaped form rather than an independent strike through de; both readings remain possible. The lower de carré and looped l survive, but the index is compressed at the clipped edge. Root also consulted the already saved first-pass close crop of this same ending.
- Insertion at bottom edge: \inserted{\unsure{de carré $l_i$}} — The words are substantially supported, but the compressed final index at the edge remains uncertain.
- cancelled opening verb: \unsure{Appliquant|Appliquons} — Appliqu is readable, but the last connected strokes meet the broad diagonal cancellation. An apparent t upstroke favors Appliquant; the covered ending is not sufficient to exclude Appliquons. Neither grammar nor the shared witnesses settles that ending.
- separate g before modulo in cancelled paragraph: \unsure{$g$|\deleted{$g$}} — After the g inside int(g) and its closing curve there is another distinct rounded head with a long J-like descender before modulo. This separate g was omitted in the first reading and in C. A continuation of the short cancellation may cross its head, so its local live/deleted state remains uncertain inside the already cancelled paragraph. The d/e/j/a sequence of déjà is readable despite the broad X.
- all three unindexed pi forms in live lower paragraphs: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — The large independent sign has a flat cap and two nearly straight legs, while the tiny sign in parentheses is too compressed to establish the same feet. These allow capital Pi as well as the local pi notation. The prefix Aut has the lowered l/a/c sequence; the comma and curved insertion leader are visible. No mathematical convention alone chooses the letter case. After dans the low article body is a, followed by c, a tall l, and connected a/s/s/e, then m/o/d. The group sign has a capped upright body with a hooked inner/right leg and does not securely distinguish pi from capital Pi. The et after sigma-i is definitely crossed. At the far right, by contrast, the long upper stroke can be the cap of a live sigma-shaped form rather than an independent strike through de; both readings remain possible. The lower de carré and looped l survive, but the index is compressed at the clipped edge. Root also consulted the already saved first-pass close crop of this same ending.
- before hatted Z after isomorphes: \unsure{à|:} — The word stops after the q descender and a small raised terminal t; no baseline uement sequence is present. The letters of isomorphes are readable. Before the hatted doubled Z, however, the mark consists of an upper point and a short lower dark stroke without a secure a loop. The accented-a reading is retained first, with a colon alternative. The final punctuation is a down-curving comma.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Cancelled epsilon_0 definition, generator index: l_{\unsure{1|0}} — The diagonal strike touches the base/subscript area; not every index stroke is plain.
- Interlinear square relation after doit écrire: \varepsilon_{\unsure{\infty|0}}^2=l_\infty — The compact subscript has a small left component and closed right portion. It may be infinity or 0; the expected later formula does not settle the ink.
- Cancelled word after je: \deleted{\unsure{trouve}} — The horizontal crossing obscures the joined middle letters.
- Added word above the cancelled trouve: \inserted{\unsure{écris}} — The visible beginning supports écris, but the final small strokes meet the right image boundary.
- Lower grouping stroke beneath the first surviving square product: TikZ drawing of long lower rule, inner upturn, and final curl — Below the product a long shallow rule starts under the first sigma, turns up beneath the second sigma, and continues with a lower curl under the final factor. A single conventional underbrace cannot faithfully express these two connected courses, so their visible shape is drawn without assigning an algebraic scope. Across the middle l0 is a short dark cross-stroke which may be a local cancellation or an intensified letter crossing; both states remain. Donc has the d ascender and a longer low continuation, rather than only de.
- Second index in the added and struck (=) inverse expression: l_{\unsure{0|\infty}}^{-1} — The low outline has a broad curved form which can be read as one 0 with the preceding l foot or as a compact infinity. The expected inverse identity is not used to decide it.
- Doubly cancelled prefix after live i.e.: \deleted{\unsure{l_\infty^{-1}l_1|l_\infty^{-1}l_\infty|l_0^{-1}l_1}} — Both l stems and the first inverse are clear. A separate dark downward trace after the second l supports lower1 more strongly than a full infinity; the horizontal cancellation still obscures the junction. The first index can be compressed infinity or0. The prefix has its own horizontal crossing in addition to the broad oblique cancellation of the line, so the two deletion levels are retained.
- Word of the right marginal query: \unsure{prends|prenons|pour} — A long p descender and low joined middle end in a dense rising/looped group. Prends remains preferred, with prenons and the shorter pour possible because the final group is not separable. Between alpha and the overwritten digit, the upper stroke has a hooked angle over a lower bar, so equals or greater-than-or-equal is possible. The digit remains2/1. The query mark is distinct. This does not change the separately documented tests of the candidate values1 and2.
- Overwritten value in the right marginal query: \alpha=\unsure{2|1} — Overlapping upright, curved entry and lower foot do not separate a unique surviving digit.
- raised sign after fixe: \unsure{6|$\sigma$} — The second line has on, the tall d and final t of doit, a distinct looped l/e before multiplier, and the multiplier ending. It is not donc en multipliant. Above the gap after fixe is a separate dark hooked sign with a closed lower loop, read provisionally6 or sigma; it is not securely attached as an exponent of the following l-infinity. The native whole page and the already saved opening crop were consulted. Its role is not identified as pagination or a footnote. The cancelled formula included in the blind excerpt is the separate B02 passage.
- cancelled donc after aura: \unsure{donc} — Aura has visible low courses. The next d survives but its middle and final courses meet the upper strike, so donc remains a proposal. The inserted lower index has a small left component and closed right component; it is not secure enough to remove0 as an alternative to infinity. The two final dark points sit beside one another horizontally, not vertically as a colon.
- three unindexed pi signs in identification paragraph: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — The initial large E has no separate accent. The terminal angular crossed stroke belongs to the ending t of Ecrivant, not an additional multiplication or triangle symbol. The g has a closed head and long hooked descender without a separate dot. Au lieu de, with the tall l and dotted i, is visible. The final capped two-legged group sign permits either pi alphabet and is qualified consistently with the following line. Je has the long j descender and low e, rather than the shorter les course. The cancelled word’s tall first stroke and joined middle are obscured, so trouve remains doubtful. Above it an accent and écr-like beginning are visible; the ending is clipped at the right edge. There is no secure p descender establishing peux or pour. The capped group signs, including the small Aut argument, do not securely settle lowercase versus capital Pi.
- cancelled comme before epsilon formula: \unsure{comme} — The c opening and low joined courses support comme, but several middle strokes intersect the cancellation. A distinct dark point stands after the cancelled formula and before the separate live epsilon equation. The latter has its own final low stop after the alpha power. Neither point is part of a crossed letter.
- central l0 in first surviving square product: \unsure{l_0|\deleted{l_0}} — Below the product a long shallow rule starts under the first sigma, turns up beneath the second sigma, and continues with a lower curl under the final factor. A single conventional underbrace cannot faithfully express these two connected courses, so their visible shape is drawn without assigning an algebraic scope. Across the middle l0 is a short dark cross-stroke which may be a local cancellation or an intensified letter crossing; both states remain. Donc has the d ascender and a longer low continuation, rather than only de.
- sign within struck inverse insertion: \unsure{(=)|(\Rightarrow)} — Aussi has a low a/u course, two separate short s strokes and final dotted i; there is no n shoulder for ainsi. The small upper insertion is crossed. Two short bars favor equals inside parentheses, but the strike/closing curve prevents excluding an arrowhead. The final lower index remains0/infinity because its wide curve is partly merged with the l foot.
- last lower index in live product before marginal query: l_{\unsure{0|\infty}}^{-1} — The initial subscript has a short rounded dark body, consistent with0. In the final parenthesized factor the lower trace joins the l foot and opens into a second rightward rise, admitting either a cursive0 or compressed infinity. The first0 remains preferred, but the formula is not used to eliminate the alternative.
- relation sign in marginal query: \unsure{=|\ge} — A long p descender and low joined middle end in a dense rising/looped group. Prends remains preferred, with prenons and the shorter pour possible because the final group is not separable. Between alpha and the overwritten digit, the upper stroke has a hooked angle over a lower bar, so equals or greater-than-or-equal is possible. The digit remains2/1. The query mark is distinct. This does not change the separately documented tests of the candidate values1 and2.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Deleted word after σ_i in first prose line: \deleted{\unsure{par}} — The p descender is plain, but the horizontal strike obscures the middle and terminal letters. This is a new certainty qualification, not a substantive change from C’s proposed word.
- Deleted word after rho in On retrouve line: \deleted{\unsure{comme}} — The open initial c and remaining low traces support comme; several letters merge with the strike. This new doubt preserves the deletion and C’s proposed word.
- Last epsilon in cancelled continuation: \unsure{\varepsilon_\infty|\varepsilon_0|\varepsilon_1} — The two short parallel vertical strokes stand beneath sigma0sigma1, untouched by the cancellation, and the crossed epsilon product sits directly below them. The first transcription separated this into an independently centered display, losing the precise attachment. The final lower trace of the last epsilon is broad and filled: a compressed infinity or0 is possible, with the earlier1 retained as a less secure alternative. No subscript is supplied from the expected product.
- Deleted superscript in marginal derivation: \deleted{\unsure{2|3}} — The small exponent has a dark oblique crossing and surviving upper and lower bends. These allow3 as well as2. The epsilon bases and their lower1/0/infinity indices remain readable; the following epsilon1-squared and epsilon-infinity-squared lie above the grouping line labelled epsilon0-to-minus2. The broader formula need not be made wholly doubtful merely because this one old exponent is obscured.
- Final word at lower edge: \unsure{élém\textsuperscript{ts}} — The abbreviation is supported, but the small raised ending does not securely distinguish plural ts from singular t.
- cancelled word after multipliant: \deleted{\unsure{dans|donc}} — The opening m consists of two low arches on the baseline; the later l/t ascenders rise well above it, so an editorial capital is not retained. The cancelled word has a readable d and terminal hooked trace, but the long strike obscures the middle; dans is preferred with donc possible. The live inverse-order wording, rho cubed, equality and1 are clear.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening insertion: cancelled stable: \deleted{\unsure{stable|stables}} — The article ends in a small e course, without a separate final hook establishing les. Groupe and envisagé end before the insertion guide, with no distinct plural s. Fixé has the terminal accented e and no following s. The old stable is traversed by a dark strike, including its right end, so an additional s there cannot be excluded. The comma before il is a small separate downward mark at the guide’s return.
- Expanded product: first factor: \unsure{l_1|l_1'} — A detached short oblique tick lies above/right of the initial factor. Its status as a prime is not decisive; the unprimed C reading remains first.
- Upper cancelled first-underbrace trial: first index: l_{\unsure{1}}\sigma_\infty — The first l is traversed by the local cancellation; a dark lower stroke favors 1 but is partly merged with the crossing.
- First live epsilon identity: last subscript: \varepsilon_{\unsure{1}}^{-1} — The final subscript is heavily inked, with a vertical foot compatible with 1. Its upper portion is a dark blot and cannot be recovered more exactly.
- Final line: cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{utiliser}} — The u/t/i/l/i sequence is visible; the later connected letters are traversed by the dark strike. The first reading utiliser is retained with local uncertainty.
- cancelled prose before unexpected sigma relation: \unsure{aussi|on a} — The broad diagonal crosses this prose line and continues toward the displayed sigma product, maintaining its cancelled state. The short course after donc has low joined humps and a separate dark final body, but a crossing stroke obscures part of the middle. Aussi is still possible; segmentation as on a cannot be excluded. The relation inattendue and the indices infinity,1,0 remain clear.
- final line after cancelled utiliser: \unsure{(et|Cat} — After the crossed utiliser is a large open left curve followed by a low loop and tall terminal stroke. These can be an opening parenthesis plus e/t, or C/a/t. There is no secure triangular A beginning for Aut, nor a closed O that establishes Out. The next sign has one straight upper bar and a separate wavy two-humped lower body, supporting barred m rather than two straight equality bars or an upper tilde. The following pour les is clear.

### Remaining unresolved places

- first display, right Pi index: \unsure{1|*} — Overwritten, branching dark strokes do not isolate either complete alternative.
- cancelled word after P, barred P: \deleted{\unsure{qui}} — A q-like loop survives, but the remaining letters and i dot are not independently clear.
- cancelled baseline verb before induisent: \deleted{\unsure{échangent}} — Beginning and g descender support the word; the cancellation covers several central and final strokes.
- inserted and then struck replacement above échangent: \inserted{\deleted{\unsure{fixent}}} — New local doubt: f, the i dot, x and the terminal t-like stroke are visible, but e/n are flattened by the horizontal strike. Agreement of C and H does not resolve them.
- cancelled word before live sur: \deleted{\unsure{resp|sur}.} — Right descender and stop favor resp.; the obscured body does not exclude C’s sur. The terminal point is certain.
- cancelled rotations below symétries: \deleted{\unsure{rotations}} — New local doubt: opening r/o, tall strokes and the terminal ons contour are recognizable, but the heavy cancellation flattens the middle a/ti sequence.
- cancelled axis notation before (0,2): \deleted{$\unsure{P,\bar P|1,\infty|0,1,\infty}$} — The inserted resp. and symétries are above the line and have separate guides/attachment. The old rotations is crossed through its medial bodies. The old axis cluster has a tall left body buried in hatching, a low comma, a second tall stroke and a lower curved terminal. These do not securely distinguish P,barred P from the proposed digit/infinity sequences. The live (0,2) and its following comma are clear.
- p.r. au/un plan: \unsure{au|un} — au is favored, but the compressed first body and shallow following strokes do not conclusively distinguish the alternatives.
- first On considère, ornate S3: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S and index 3 are clear; the exact TeX alphabet remains a representational uncertainty.
- cet ordre, et ornate S3 times Z/2: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Rounded outer sweep and returning inner curve do not settle the supplied typographic alternatives.
- membership following the later dotted sigma list: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The S identity and 3 are clear; the exact alphabet remains unresolved.
- raised and struck extension label: \inserted{$\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal E_0}}$} — Crossed and overwritten E-like strokes with a low closed index support E0. The exact index and stroke sequence remain uncertain, although insertion and cancellation are recorded.
- product beginning the last prose line: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Same local ornate S form; neither supplied alphabet is decisively distinguished.
- upper U subscript in final display: \deleted{\unsure{0,3}} — The cancellation crosses both small digit bodies; the lower replacement U0,3 is clear but does not license resolving the old subscript.
- product under the final display brace: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Identity S3 clear; typographic alphabet left open.
- cancelled verb after On: \deleted{\unsure{pourra|posera}} — On is outside the strike. The old word has a long p descender, a broad rounded second body, low medial loops and a terminal a-like body. A heavy horizontal strike traverses the medial letters and their joins; the two small r bodies of pourra cannot be isolated securely against the s/e sequence of posera.
- live ending after crossed échangent/fixent: \unsure{induisent|deux $p^t$} — Each raised sigma has its own dot and the low indices0,1,infinity. The guide returns before de. The doubled projective letters remain visible. Échangent and the separately raised fixent are traversed by cancellation, so their doubts remain. At the right edge, the compact live sequence has an ascender, low connected bodies and a tall terminal; its crowded opening and segmentation do not securely distinguish induisent from deux followed by a p/t abbreviation.
- before cet ordre: \unsure{dans|ds} — After the tuple is a clear d-like ascender/loop followed by a long low joined course. The a/n bodies are compressed into that course, so an abbreviated ds cannot be excluded. Cet ordre and the following ornate-S product are clear; the Z has a second parallel stroke.
- first numerator in the oblique sigma0 formula: \unsure{z|1} — After the minus the small numerator has a flat upper stroke, a steep central descent and a lower foot. Its compact form can be z or a footed1; it is less clearly diagonal than the adjacent denominator z. The following equality z/(z-1), the other four maps and the absence of terminal punctuation are clear. Algebraic consistency supports the preferred z but is not treated as proof of the ink.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Initial cancelled word after Malgoire: \deleted{\unsure{correspondant|comprenant}} — Middle letters are crossed; the late descender favours the first reading but does not establish every letter.
- Initial tuple second component: \unsure{l|\lambda} — Tall upper loop favours l; anomalous left branch and retracing permit lambda.
- First fibre subscript in the inline map: U'_{\unsure{a|x}} — Oval with right downstroke favours a; the small retraced shape does not safely exclude x.
- Within cancelled stable phrase, between par and la: \unsure{à|$\dot\tau$} — The raised replacement begins with low e/n/s bodies followed by a separate stop, then des, the compact p/t/s abbreviation, and fixes with f and crossed x. It does not begin with the deep p and tall terminal t of passant. The old stable/par/la/symétrie bodies remain identifiable through their horizontal strike. Between par and la, a low dark body lies on that strike below a detached short upper mark: à and dotted tau remain possible.
- p.r. ... plan: \unsure{au|à un|à} — The accented initial body and its low continuation are followed by another compact low course before the large strokes of plan. The spacing and joined strokes admit à un as well as the previously recorded au/à segmentation, but do not securely settle it. In the later parenthesis, the small oval followed by a shallow joined u course supports ou rather than a mathematical dot-minus operator. Fixant, both P labels and a_i are clear.
- Before passant: \unsure{ne|un|en} — The closing parenthesis after a_j,a_k is followed by blank paper and et; there is no separate comma. The next short word has two compressed low courses, permitting ne, un or en. Passant is followed on the next baseline by the clear pas par a_i; pas is not omitted. The old short word has an A-like initial, so Au remains tentative. The inserted parenthesis reads pour1 membership Gamma and its guide joins before les, after the three l labels.
- Cancelled word before Désignant: \deleted{\unsure{Au}} — A-like initial and u-like ending remain crossed and entangled.
- Destination list after en allant vers: \unsure{a_0,a_1,a_\infty|0,1,\infty} — All three forms are retraced with a-like loops; the separate before/after states cannot be securely recovered.
- Middle factor in relation (1): \unsure{l'_1|l_1} — A separate high tick can be a prime, but an incidental mark remains possible.
- Between ! and On peut l’écrire: \unsure{\}|?|)} — After the clear exclamation mark there is a separate narrow sign with an upper loop, long descending middle and a lower fork/return. No detached question-mark dot is visible. Its overall contour is compatible with a closing brace, while an irregular question mark or parenthesis remains possible. On peut l’écrire and all three minus-one exponents are independently clear.
- oblique mark after path l before de P: \unsure{/|)} — After the looped l is one long oblique stroke that curves at both ends. Its downward-left slope favours a slash; its curved course also permits a closing parenthesis. It is visibly separate from de and cannot be omitted. The inverse exponent on gamma and the basepoint P are clear.
- underlined sign before gauche: \unsure{à|:} — Before gauche are a short low curved body and a separate upper dark point. They can be the compressed a and accent of à, but do not exclude a colon. The underline extends beneath this sign as well as gauche; the descending g crosses it without cancelling the word.
- between P and barred P in semicircle description: \unsure{à|:} — Between P and barred P are two compact dark marks, vertically separated, which allow either a compressed accented à or a colon. The cancelled word has l, apostrophe, low u/n/i bodies, a q descender and final u/e course: l’unique, rather than the different ascenders and length of équateur/hémisphère. Demi- has d, connected e/m/i and a separate end dash. The raised replacement reads celui des.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Upper display, first cancelled old product: \unsure{l_0\rho} — The loops and lower index are traversed by heavy local strokes.
- Upper display, second cancelled old product: \unsure{l_1\rho} — The narrow proposed index and rho body are blackened by the cancellation.
- First marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_\infty} — The sigma body and proposed infinity are overlaid by dense hatching.
- First marginal trial, parenthetic product: \unsure{l_0\sigma_0\sigma_0\rho^{-1}|\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}} — Ces begins with a low open c, not a tall l; the left-pointing arrow and three points after the conjugation example are distinct. Raised2 exponents remain visible after the first two parenthesized trial factors, contrary to their omission in the blind reading. The crowded first trial’s final parenthesis admits both recorded factor sequences. Beside the second live equality, the crossed inscription has a large closed O, low n and separate a, matching the live On a immediately below; it is not sigma-infinity. In the second cancelled action trial, the final low index has two joined rounded traces, favouring infinity over0, but the hatch crosses it. The tall left boundary is a real independent rule and stops near the second live action. All three replacement action formulas are clear and unstruck.
- Second marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_1} — A sigma-like top is visible but its index is obscured by local crossing strokes.
- Third marginal trial, trailing sigma: \unsure{\sigma_\infty} — The trailing body and subscript are crossed; every component cannot be certified.
- Small side trial beside second marginal row, left member: \unsure{l_0} — The small looped l and lower round trace are crowded into the side of the row.
- Small side trial beside second marginal row, right member: \unsure{\rho^{-1}\sigma_0} — The compact superscript and final index are entangled with crossing strokes.
- Third cancelled marginal action, right member: \unsure{l_1^{-1}l_0^{-1}} — The product is small and struck; the exponent strokes cannot all be separated from the cancellation.
- Large cancelled argument, puisque: \unsure{puisque} — The compact word is compatible with puisque, but its middle is crossed and not every letter is plain.
- Lower cancelled word: \unsure{distincts|abstrait\textsuperscript{t}} — The opening loop and tall stroke favor d and the short terminal sequence favors distincts; the horizontal strike still masks medial letters.
- Group letter after présentation de in the large cancellation: \unsure{\Pi|\pi} — Tall two-legged outline favors Pi, but the curved right foot leaves case locally uncertain.
- Short horizontal marks surrounding la relation: \mbox{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(relation.base)]
\node[inner sep=0pt,outer sep=0pt] (relation) {la relation};
\draw ([xshift=0.05em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=0.77em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.49em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.21em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.93em,yshift=0.35ex]relation.north west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=0.40em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.12em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=1.84em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\draw ([xshift=2.56em,yshift=-0.35ex]relation.south west) -- ++(0.42em,0);
\end{tikzpicture}} — Five short upper strokes and four lower strokes are preserved geometrically; their function is not readable from the local arrangement.
- Live et sigma, exact attachment: \inserted{et $\sigma$} — The text is readable and live; its elevated position does not uniquely specify the scope of replacement.
- M0826: \unsure{1|0} — The second index is narrow and bent, favoring 1, but less distinctly formed than the first.
- M0827: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The large ornate S has a rounded outer sweep and hooked upper end; this local glyph does not securely distinguish the two typographic alphabets.
- M0828: \unsure{Comme on voit} — The first line starts with a large C and compressed connected strokes compatible with Comme on voit. The middle line has three separated groups: the first permits ils or être, the middle has two low humps permitting en/un, and the last permits sont/des. The last line has pas with a distinct underline, followed by crowded letters admitting conjugués or exigée. A separate-looking short terminal horizontal stroke supports an underline below the last syllable, but its independence from the writing remains uncertain and is retained as an alternative. The nearby dotted-rho cube belongs to the main display, not this prose.
- M0829: \unsure{ils|être} \unsure{en|un} \unsure{sont|des} — The first line starts with a large C and compressed connected strokes compatible with Comme on voit. The middle line has three separated groups: the first permits ils or être, the middle has two low humps permitting en/un, and the last permits sont/des. The last line has pas with a distinct underline, followed by crowded letters admitting conjugués or exigée. A separate-looking short terminal horizontal stroke supports an underline below the last syllable, but its independence from the writing remains uncertain and is retained as an alternative. The nearby dotted-rho cube belongs to the main display, not this prose.
- M0830: \unsure{\uline{pas} conjugu\uline{és}|\uline{pas} conjugués|\uline{pas} exig\uline{ée}} — The first line starts with a large C and compressed connected strokes compatible with Comme on voit. The middle line has three separated groups: the first permits ils or être, the middle has two low humps permitting en/un, and the last permits sont/des. The last line has pas with a distinct underline, followed by crowded letters admitting conjugués or exigée. A separate-looking short terminal horizontal stroke supports an underline below the last syllable, but its independence from the writing remains uncertain and is retained as an alternative. The nearby dotted-rho cube belongs to the main display, not this prose.
- M0831: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The final ornate S repeats the broad rounded and hooked outline, without a decisive local alphabet distinction.
- M0832: on \unsure{a} \unsure{pas|plus|puis} $\unsure{\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}=\sigma_1}$ (?) — The note begins C’est faux with a clear comma. The next line begins a large C, and before relations the article starts with a low open c rather than an ascender l. After rho=1, on is clear and a remains crowded. The following short word has a deep p and low a/s-like ending, favouring pas, with plus/puis retained. The next line contains a looped rho, a roofed sigma with low0, another descending rho with high inverse strokes, a two-stroke equality and a final sigma/index cluster; these are compatible with rho sigma0 rho^-1=sigma1, not a securely readable groupe word. The compact question mark has a hook and lower point enclosed by two curved parentheses. Overlap with the main writing prevents resolving the full formula.
- second product in system(2), first sigma high dot: \unsure{\sigma_1|\dot\sigma_1} — The three old l-rho products are crossed locally, with replacement rho-l products below/above them. The second product’s first sigma has a small dark detached point above it, separated from its roof; it may be a dot or an incidental mark. The square exponents and indices in the top row are clear. Display alignment syntax does not change the reading.
- second cancelled marginal action, right-member index: \unsure{l_\infty|l_0} — Ces begins with a low open c, not a tall l; the left-pointing arrow and three points after the conjugation example are distinct. Raised2 exponents remain visible after the first two parenthesized trial factors, contrary to their omission in the blind reading. The crowded first trial’s final parenthesis admits both recorded factor sequences. Beside the second live equality, the crossed inscription has a large closed O, low n and separate a, matching the live On a immediately below; it is not sigma-infinity. In the second cancelled action trial, the final low index has two joined rounded traces, favouring infinity over0, but the hatch crosses it. The tall left boundary is a real independent rule and stops near the second live action. All three replacement action formulas are clear and unstruck.
- trivial path, sign between P and P: \unsure{à|:} — Trivial has a tall initial t, r/v turns and a terminal l; de has the small d loop and following e. Between the two P labels are two compact vertically separated marks, compatible with a compressed accented à or a colon. The second rho has a separate high dot; no hyphen is written in au dessus.
- end of large cancellation, relations abbreviation: \unsure{relations|rel.} — The displayed rho equality has its own horizontal strike inside the larger cancelled block. The broad diagonal strokes end on the left after the continuation containing sigma_i squared; il faudra que rho soit lies to their right and is live. After des, the visible r/e/l bodies are followed by a compressed low course and a small terminal mark; full relations and abbreviated rel. cannot be securely separated.
- crossed conjunction before unfinished sigma equality: \unsure{ou|or} — Produit de termes and pas deux consécutifs have no crossing deletion stroke; the latter phrase has an independent underline. The short terminal turn of pas supports s. A separate horizontal strike crosses the old doubtful word and the unfinished sigma equality, leaving its following comma below the line. The small word before the equality has a rounded o and a short final return compatible with u or r. Et sigma is raised above/right of the crossed equality and remains live.
- after dotted presentation’s ornate S3: \unsure{),|,} — The first index is one narrow upright with a foot and no closed oval. The second index is smaller and bent, so its1/0 doubt remains. High dots are present over all geometric symbols. The final ornate S has the same unresolved alphabet form. Its following curved terminal mark is clear, but separation into closing-parenthesis-plus-comma versus one comma is not secure in this crop.
- after references(1)(2), final word: \unsure{outre|enfin|entre} — After et, a low rounded opening and joined course lead to a tall stroke and short final r/e-like turn. There is no independently clear f descender or i dot establishing enfin. Outre is favoured, while enfin and entre remain possible. The preceding système, des relations and references(1)(2) are clear.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Left margin, cancelled product: \deleted{$\unsure{[\underset{\sigma_\infty}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\sigma_0\rho^{-1}][\underset{\sigma_1}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\underset{\sigma_\infty}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho^{-1}\sigma_0$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\rho^{-1}]|[\underset{\sigma_\infty}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\sigma_0\rho^{-1}][\underset{\sigma_1}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho\sigma_0\rho^{-1}$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\underset{\sigma_\infty}{\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(term.base)]\node[inner sep=1pt] (term) {$\rho^{-1}\sigma_0\rho$};\draw ([yshift=-1pt]term.south west) -- ++(0,-3pt) -| ([yshift=-1pt]term.south east);\end{tikzpicture}}\rho^{-1}]}$} — The first square bracket and the adjacent close/open boundary are visible despite diagonal hatching. Below its initial rho^-1 sigma0 rho group there is an independent short flat rule with a sigma-infinity label; that first annotation was omitted. Two further flat-ended rules carry sigma1 and sigma-infinity. The latter endpoint and a possible additional rho remain entangled, so both existing alternatives are retained. The high inverse above the next rho has short minus/upright strokes, favouring the first reading over the blind un-inverted sequence. No =1 is written beyond the terminal bracket. The rules are represented literally as flat brackets, not detached empty underbraces.

- Final prose, notation singular/plural: \unsure{notation|notations} — The abbreviation ends in a connected small s-like turn followed by white space, with no separate point. Notation has its t ascenders and long final course; the short last hook could be an exit or an additional s, so singular/plural remains open. Indiqués and donnent retain their q descender, dotted i and final t.
- System(8), cancellation scope: \[
\deleted{(8)}\quad\left\{\begin{aligned}
\unsure{\deleted{\varepsilon_0}|\varepsilon_0}&=l_0^{\deleted{\unsure{-1|4}}}\sigma_1 l_{\unsure{0|1}}^{\deleted{\unsure{-1|4}}}\\
\unsure{\deleted{\varepsilon_1}|\varepsilon_1}&=\unsure{\deleted{l_\infty^{-1}}|l_\infty^{-1}}\sigma_\infty \deleted{l_1^{-1}}\ \inserted{l_1}\\
\varepsilon_\infty&=l_0^{-1}\sigma_0 l_\infty
\end{aligned}\right.
\] — The relation number is crossed by several long oblique strokes. Other large strokes pass through the first two left-member labels and the second row’s initial factor; their exact cancellation scope remains uncertain and is encoded locally. Both first-row high clusters are heavily crossed, admitting inverse-looking-1 or4-like remnants. The second row’s final l1^-1 has a clear long crossing stroke; a separate l with low1 is written farther right and slightly below as its replacement. That complete l1 body is not an exponent attached to the third row. The third row’s final l-infinity has no independent high inverse strokes and no cancellation crosses that entire row. A blanket whole-system deletion is therefore replaced by explicit local states and a note about uncertain overall scope.

### Remaining unresolved places

- M0843: \overset{\deleted{\unsure{\sim}}}{\unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3}} — The ornate alphabet remains unresolved. A separate heavily cancelled upper mark is present; its underlying tilde is retained as doubtful.
- M0844: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\tilde{\mathfrak S}_3|\mathcal S_3|\tilde{\mathcal S}_3} — The upper loop of S and nearby groupe descender prevent a secure independent-tilde reading. Both alphabet and adornment alternatives are retained, preferring the unadorned local outline.
- M0845: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\tilde{\mathfrak S}_3|\mathcal S_3|\tilde{\mathcal S}_3} — The margin has an ornate S3, but neighboring strokes and the letter's own loop do not isolate a definite tilde.
- M0846: \unsure{(} — Curved strokes before en overlap the slanted margin; a distinct opening parenthesis cannot be separated with certainty.
- Cancelled parenthesis in (13): \unsure{=\rho} — The equals-like strokes and descending rho are partly recoverable, but the scribble crosses the upper letter body and bar region.
- Cancelled point-name before live i: \unsure{a_i|\infty|a} — The sigma inside phi has an independent dot and a separate crossed upper mark. The r of p.r. is a short rising shoulder. Before live dotted i the old cluster is so heavily retraced that a_i, a linked infinity-like pair, and a simple a remain possible. The l0 at the end of the preceding line and the written en before the inverse are not an arrow or les lacets. The locally doubled l includes a crossed first stem. The following overwritten index remains1/0.
- First ornate S, identity of the cancelled upper mark: \deleted{\unsure{\sim}} — The separate mark is certainly cancelled, but its underlying complete shape is not exposed.
- Main loop-image sentence, first right member after l-infinity: l_{\unsure{1|0}}^{-1} — A narrow dark upright overlies a small loop. One is preferred, but the old rounded component prevents full exclusion of zero.
- Introduction to (15), word after à: \unsure{sv|savoir} — Only a highly compressed returning stroke and short upper run are clear; a spelled-out word and an abbreviation cannot be distinguished securely.
- group sign after sur: \unsure{\pi|\Pi} — The first group sign has two straight descending posts beneath a transverse cap. In the second sign the right post turns into a distinct curved foot; lowercase pi is plausible, but the full cap and close scale prevent a secure case ruling. A small isolated low point follows sur before the sign. It is retained tentatively rather than silently interpreted as punctuation or discarded as incidental ink. The closed a indices and capital On remain clear.
- point after sur: \unsure{.} — The first group sign has two straight descending posts beneath a transverse cap. In the second sign the right post turns into a distinct curved foot; lowercase pi is plausible, but the full cap and close scale prevent a secure case ruling. A small isolated low point follows sur before the sign. It is retained tentatively rather than silently interpreted as punctuation or discarded as incidental ink. The closed a indices and capital On remain clear.
