### Remaining unresolved places

- M0847: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Gamma identity in prose: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0848: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (11), source of phi: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0849: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (11), factor after Gamma=: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- M0850: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — (12), source of the group-to-E arrow: the large sign has a broad outer loop, an upper returning stroke and an inner curling lower stroke, with a distinct subscript 3. It is consistently ornate S-shaped here, but its ductus alone does not determine a modern fraktur versus calligraphic alphabet.
- (10), cancelled prefix before the first product: \deleted{\unsure{\varepsilon_0}} — Before the first live product’s epsilon_1, the crossed compact body and low round trace support epsilon_0; superimposed strokes prevent resolution of the complete cancelled sign.
- (10), second product, post-rho epsilon subscript: \varepsilon_{\unsure{1|\infty}}^2 — A left loop and a dark descending right stem coexist in the retraced subscript. Neither layer can be isolated with confidence.
- (10), first product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.
- (10), second product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.
- (10), third product, cancelled rho before the last factor: \deleted{\unsure{\rho}} — The long descender remains visible; the head is covered by several cancellation strokes. The rho reading is plausible but not fully legible.
- Lower margin, cancelled sign: \deleted{$\unsure{\tilde\rho}$} — The lower marginal cancellation leaves a wavy upper trace and a descended looped sign, but its head is obscured by several crossing strokes and the connector.
- Upper margin, terminal nonverbal mark: \begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.13cm,y=0.13cm,baseline=-0.5ex]
\draw[line width=0.35pt] (0,0) .. controls (0.3,1.1) and (0.0,2.6) .. (0.45,3.6) .. controls (0.65,3.9) and (0.95,3.9) .. (1.2,3.7);
\draw[line width=0.5pt] (0,0.1) -- (0.2,-0.1) -- (0.25,0.5) -- (0.6,0.2);
\draw[line width=0.4pt] (0.95,2.2) .. controls (0.8,2.5) and (1.3,2.6) .. (1.4,2.25) .. controls (1.2,2.1) and (1.05,2.1) .. (0.95,2.2);
\draw[line width=0.45pt] (-0.45,-0.1) .. controls (-0.25,0.1) and (-0.2,0.4) .. (-0.25,0.6);
\end{tikzpicture} — The hooked stem, upper loop and dark foot are visible and reproduced; their lexical or referential function is not determined.
- Lower margin, broad left hook and sloping line: \begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.18cm,y=0.18cm,baseline=-0.5ex]
\draw[line width=0.35pt] (-0.5,2.8) .. controls (-1.0,1.7) and (-0.7,1.0) .. (0.25,0.4) .. controls (0.5,0.2) and (0.55,0.0) .. (0.4,-0.2);
\draw[line width=0.35pt] (5.7,2.1) .. controls (3.9,1.65) and (2.0,1.5) .. (1.0,1.3) .. controls (0.55,1.2) and (0.4,0.4) .. (0.15,-0.2);
\draw[line width=0.5pt] (0.15,-0.2) -- (0.55,0.1) -- (0.6,0.5);
\end{tikzpicture} — The line changes direction toward the cancelled sign. Its attachment argues against emphasis, but no unambiguous arrow label or paired bracket fixes its exact function.
- word after expliciter: \unsure{direct$\bar m$t|directement} — The final word begins with a looped d and short dotted/connected course consistent with direct, and ends with a tall t-like stroke. A separate long horizontal abbreviation bar lies above the low m-like middle. The two tall b/l strokes required by d’emblée are not visible. The compressed interior still prevents a letter-perfect recovery, so the abbreviated form is preferred with directement retained as an alternative; no expansion is silently imposed.
- Gamma identity, lower separator: \unsure{,|.} — The point-name before dans has a rounded upper bowl on a straight stem, supporting P. Between the small0 and3 the dark short mark has insufficient extension to establish comma rather than point. Both sigma and tau have separate dots; the old On is crossed by a continuous horizontal stroke. The integer symbol has the doubled writing represented by blackboard Z. The ornate S alphabet remains unresolved.
- two points below phi in(11): \unsure{:} — Two separate dark points lie beneath the end of phi’s descender, at a different position from the clear colon to its right. They may be a colon-like inscription or incidental pen traces, so their identity remains qualified. The upper arrow has a plain tail; the lower arrow has a short vertical tail bar. The target has a comma between dotted sigma and sg but no outer parentheses, and the first row has no terminal comma.

### Remaining unresolved places

- cancelled word before avec: \deleted{\unsure{relati.|relat.}} — The first quoted word has a descending g, tall l and accented final é. The second has a p descender, low o/n sequence and distinct rising d before its accented ending, excluding profini. Malgoire has the capital M, small a, tall l, descending g and o/i/r/e sequence. Gén ends without a separate point. The cancelled short form exposes r/e/l/t-like strokes and a high point, but the final i stem is entangled with the strike, so relati. and relat. remain possible.
- (17), first row, first overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{0}} — The live infinity covers the old index.
- (17), first row, second overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{\infty|0}} — The first cancelled tau’s cap and lower curve are heavily crossed while its lower1 remains visible; the complete tau1 factor is therefore qualified. The next cancelled tau-infinity and equals strokes are clearer. On the live right side the first infinity has two dark current lobes over an old tentative0. The next current1 is a thick upright, while the earlier low trace admits infinity or0. In the second row the current0 is a dark closed oval with a paler former loop extending left; the earlier infinity remains tentative. No complete cyclic pattern is supplied to settle these older layers.
- (17), second row, first overwritten right-hand index: \deleted{\unsure{\infty}} — The new dark zero overlies most of the old form.

- (17), first cancelled factor at upper left: \unsure{\tau_1} — The first cancelled tau’s cap and lower curve are heavily crossed while its lower1 remains visible; the complete tau1 factor is therefore qualified. - Second intersection formula, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Upper marginal product, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Lower marginal product, ornate group sign: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — Ornate S-shaped ink and the subscript3 are present; the precise font alphabet cannot be inferred from mathematical meaning.
- Opening insertion, words: \unsure{qui contient} — Contient is favored over the discarded centralise alternative, but the compressed last letters remain insufficiently separate.

- First intersection, complete right replacement term: \unsure{\Gamma_{\unsure{0,3}}|\mathord{\text{\begin{tikzpicture}[

  \draw (.16,0)--(.16,1.40)--(.58,1.40);
\end{tikzpicture}}}} — Thick retracing can resemble a doubled left wall; the lower cluster may be a subscript or part of the overwritten material.
- Separate cancelled NB: \deleted{\uline{\unsure{NB}}} — Letter outlines remain under dense diagonal cancellation; retain the word doubt.
- Hatted map letter in L_0 definition: \unsure{\hat k_0|\hat\kappa_0} — The long ascender and two arms favor k, but the local hand does not decisively exclude kappa; the circumflex and0 index are clear.
- g_i in (21): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_infinity in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_1 in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- g_0 in (22): \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Cancellation is the preferred state because of extra angular/upward strokes. The underlying minus and1 remain occluded, and a live inverse cannot be excluded from ink alone.
- Lower marginal product, overwritten letter: \deleted{\unsure{\Gamma}} — The NB baseline is level with the lower double-hatted Gamma and superset relation. Beneath the lower ornate S there is a thin straighter descending trace with a short cap-like remnant, separate from its rounded current body; this admits an overwritten Gamma but does not certify it. The cup has a joined lower curve. Each exclamation has a raised upright and a distinct lower point beside the hatted group, so it remains a superscript rather than baseline prose punctuation. The words j’ignore si cette inclusion est une égalité and the final subset expression remain as first read.
- Final sentence, topological-generation adverb: \unsure{topolog\textsuperscript{t}|topologiquement} — The word has a clear topo/l/o/g course, then a long nearly level connecting stroke ending in a short raised t-like terminal. Separate letters for the whole iquement ending are not plain. The short form topolog with raised t is therefore preferred, while the compressed full word remains an alternative. The group before it has a cap and two complete legs; the right foot’s turn does not remove that capital structure. The final scalar again has the p stem and upper head.

### Remaining unresolved places

- U025-01: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — First preliminary formula, g_i in the left v(int(...)) term: small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-02: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — First preliminary formula, g_i in the right int(v(...)) term: small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-03: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Second preliminary formula, h_i defining v(l_i): small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-04: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — Second preliminary formula, h_i defining v(l_i)^p: small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-05: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — (23), g_i inside v: small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-06: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{-1}}|\unsure{-1}} — (23), following h_i: small black angular extra strokes favor cancellation. The underlying minus1 is not fully legible; a live exponent remains possible, so both state and glyph uncertainty are explicit.
- U025-07: \deleted{\unsure{(h_i v(g_i))^{-1}}} — The entire upper annotation is diagonally hatched. h_i v(g_i) is supported, but the letter intersections and tiny final exponent do not permit complete resolution.
- U025-08: \deleted{\unsure{par le couple|par les couples|par le groupe}} — The live word’s long initial loop can be read as the earlier g or as an f-like entry; its pale later downstroke and compressed ending do not securely distinguish groupe, formé and formée. The crossed preceding phrase exposes par and a short le/les run, while the old noun’s ending is under the strike, allowing the second reader’s plural couples without resolving it. Above des is a separate dark loop rising at left and turning up to the right with a short lower stem; its shape is drawn without assigning it a letter or a mathematical function. The extension prefix is a broad plain S, and the separate wave above two hats remains locally qualified.
- U025-09: \deleted{\unsure{\mathcal E}} — The crossed looped sign before the hatted Pi is E-like, but its exact base/upper decoration cannot be recovered fully.
- U025-10: \deleted{\unsure{\tilde v(\tilde u_i(\tilde u))\unsure{\cdot|:}\gamma_i(\tilde v)|\tilde v(\gamma_i(\tilde u))\unsure{\cdot|:}\gamma_i(\tilde v)}} — The baseline right member is crossed by one continuous stroke. Its central separator has a low point and a second short dark trace above, so dot and colon remain possible under the cancellation. The upper-left trial is independently hatched, and the upper-right expression survives beside a curved attachment line. Only one clear close follows each tilded variable. The stroke before the last tilded v is narrow and partly joined to the adjacent gamma course; its reading as an opening parenthesis is now qualified. The preferred literal expression still has an unmatched outer opening; no balancing close is inserted.
- U025-11: \inserted{\deleted{\unsure{\gamma_i(\tilde v)\cdot v(\gamma_i(\tilde u))}}} — The upper inserted formula is heavily hatched; the surviving fragments support C’s reading without resolving every letter.
- U025-12: \deleted{$\unsure{\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal E}}^!}$} — The old group name is crossed on the baseline below its S-prefixed replacement, with its accents and base obscured.
- U025-13: \deleted{\unsure{produit du groupe}} — Repeated crossing strokes obscure the baseline phrase; produit du groupe remains a provisional reading beneath the live opposé au groupe.
- M0866: \unsure{(\hat{\mathbb Z})^{\{0,1,\infty\}}|(\hat{\mathbb Z})^3} — The final Z-like body and its immediate delimiters are retraced. The exponent reaches the page edge and contains compressed marks that favor the longer indexed form but do not securely distinguish it from the shorter3 reading. Small lower traces are recorded separately without inventing a layer order.
- Opening, exponent of l_i: \unsure{\alpha_i|\beta_i} — The two upright strokes above g_i occupy the separate header position and have the spacing and height of11; they are not attached primes. Multiplication is a long low connected word after défini with no extra à. The exponent over l_i has a dense double-loop remnant and a separate small index. The nearby parenthetical alpha_i is clearer, but does not resolve whether the retraced exponent is alpha or beta, so both remain possible. The visible gauche and él. are unchanged.
- End of inserted gardant p, et en: \unsure{,} — A small detached hook follows the inserted en. It is comma-like but not securely punctuation; it is represented only as doubtful.
- After des, before crossed couples: \unsure{.} — A small isolated dark point is present. Its position and nearby crossing baselines do not establish punctuation; it is not promoted to a certain stop.
- First S-extension name in the definition, upper mark: \unsure{\sim|\deleted{\sim}} — A dark wavy trace is separate from the two small hats. Its glyph/state and, where nearby writing intrudes, exact attachment remain uncertain. No analogous mark is supplied to the other group-name occurrences.
- On a sur S-extension, upper mark: \unsure{\sim|\deleted{\sim}} — A dark wavy trace is separate from the two small hats. Its glyph/state and, where nearby writing intrudes, exact attachment remain uncertain. No analogous mark is supplied to the other group-name occurrences.

Its glyph/state and, where nearby writing intrudes, exact attachment remain uncertain. No analogous mark is supplied to the other group-name occurrences.

- Autext argument in the final sentence: \widehat{\unsure{\pi|\Pi}} — This sign is smaller and more compressed than the clearly tall two-leg Pi at the left of the final product. The right foot is curved; local case remains open.
- Right-hand hatted group factor in the final product: \widehat{\unsure{\pi|\Pi}} — This sign is smaller and more compressed than the clearly tall two-leg Pi at the left of the final product. The right foot is curved; local case remains open.
- Small lower strokes beneath final Z-like factor: \begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.10cm,y=0.10cm,baseline=0ex]

The crossed preceding phrase exposes par and a short le/les run, while the old noun’s ending is under the strike, allowing the second reader’s plural couples without resolving it. Above des is a separate dark loop rising at left and turning up to the right with a short lower stem; its shape is drawn without assigning it a letter or a mathematical function. The extension prefix is a broad plain S, and the separate wave above two hats remains locally qualified.
- M0865, opening before last tilded v in surviving(24): \tilde v(\gamma_i(\tilde u)\gamma_i\unsure{(}\tilde v) — The baseline right member is crossed by one continuous stroke. Its central separator has a low point and a second short dark trace above, so dot and colon remain possible under the cancellation. The stroke before the last tilded v is narrow and partly joined to the adjacent gamma course; its reading as an opening parenthesis is now qualified. ### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening ornate S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S|\mathcal S} — The pen form is ornate and looped; assigning a typographical alphabet would go beyond the distinguishable ink.
- Opening S subscript left delimiter: \unsure{[|\{} — The short downstroke and foot favor a bracket, but its top is partly entangled with the S flourish. The right delimiter is clearly ].
- Underbrace S3 alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The small ornamental S form is compatible with both alphabet labels.
- Cancelled phrase after conjugaison: \deleted{\unsure{au dessus des}} — The strike and a descender cross the central and final letters; the whole phrase therefore remains doubtful.
- Cancelled sign before first invariant-list tau: \unsure{\deleted{\unsure{\dot\rho^2}}|{\unsure{\dot\rho|\rho}}^{\deleted{\unsure{2}}}} — The rho-like head before the first tau is densely retraced, while its long descender survives. A separate high curved/angled remnant to its right can be read as2. The ink does not securely distinguish cancellation of the whole rho-squared prefix from cancellation of the raised2 alone, so both states are explicit; the whole-prefix cancellation remains preferred. The dot on rho is also uncertain. The later undotted rho has no dot above its open loop. Sont is a small separate insertion after that rho, while the guide under savoir phi(S3) returns after ordre6, not before P. In pts, the small t/s letters stand above the p baseline and are set raised.
- Inserted sont after live rho: \unsure{sont} — Compressed middle strokes and a large final crossing prevent a fully certain lexical reading.
- Inserted savoir phi(S3): S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The pen form does not decide the alphabet; insertion placement and numeral are clear.
- De phi(S3): S alphabet: \unsure{\mathfrak S_3|\mathcal S_3} — The broad ornamental S cannot settle the conventional alphabet name.
- Cancelled conj: \deleted{\unsure{conj}} — The inner strokes remain covered; no final abbreviation stop is independently visible.
- Cancelled qui/et after E: \deleted{\unsure{qui|et}} — After Pbar there is no separate punctuation before the small curved initial s of s’obtiennent and its high apostrophe. The hook previously assigned as a comma belongs to that initial word, so it is not duplicated. Both élémt forms have compressed middle strokes and a terminal t rather than a fully written ment sequence. The old word before live qui has a loop and dense cross-strokes that allow qui or et. The later soit/sont remains uncertain; au has a low rounded a and u before the separate dessus. In the savoir list the middle sigma’s small index is a dark upright mass favoring1, now locally qualified.
- Live soit/sont after qui: \unsure{soit|sont} — The middle upright/connection is retraced and does not decide i versus n.
- Cancelled sigma0,tau formula after prendre: \deleted{$\unsure{\sigma_0,\tau}$} — Both symbols and especially the first index are crossed; uncertainty is retained over the complete formula.
- Word before produits: les/leurs and retraced ending: \unsure{les|leurs} — The first word has a high l, a small e loop and a dark compressed ending that permits les or leurs; no second preceding token is visible. After par, the cancelled group begins with a small closed rounded head and has a low continuing turn beneath the long strike. That supports ou or un more directly than the former tau-only recovery, although the same bar and downturn still permit tau. The following rho and rho-squared are live, followed by three close points and a separate farther stop.
- Cancelled short word or symbol before rho: \deleted{\unsure{ou|un|$\tau$}} — The first word has a high l, a small e loop and a dark compressed ending that permits les or leurs; no second preceding token is visible. After par, the cancelled group begins with a small closed rounded head and has a low continuing turn beneath the long strike. That supports ou or un more directly than the former tau-only recovery, although the same bar and downturn still permit tau. The following rho and rho-squared are live, followed by three close points and a separate farther stop.
- Cancelled terminal s after Le: Le\deleted{\unsure{s}} — The cancellation occupies the terminal-s position, but the original small contour is entangled.
- Cancelled raised s after pt abbreviation: p\textsuperscript{t\deleted{\unsure{s}}} — Le has a crossed terminal letter in the s position, with seul written above the following gap. The p has a long lower stem; its t is smaller and raised at the right, with a short crossed/retraced final s-shaped trace at the same raised level. Both underlying s letters remain qualified. Sigma0, the numeral2, the two C2 factors, tau and the unfinished last sentence are unchanged.
- Underbrace label before ornate S3: \unsure{=|\simeq} — Before the small ornate S3 label, the relation has a short dark point/stroke and a longer low stroke, insufficient to exclude a weak upper wave; equals remains preferred with simeq as an alternative. The lower label’s second letter has two sides joined below, unlike the clear crossed X on the next lines, so U=U0,3 is retained. All three Pi signs have a cap and two full descending posts. The base point has a separate minus, long j stem with lower loop and one upper bar, with no extra dot; dotless j under the bar is a serialization correction. The final delimiter has a middle kink and lower foot and remains a right brace despite the opening parenthesis.
- Later savoir list, middle sigma index: \unsure{1} — After Pbar there is no separate punctuation before the small curved initial s of s’obtiennent and its high apostrophe. The hook previously assigned as a comma belongs to that initial word, so it is not duplicated. Both élémt forms have compressed middle strokes and a terminal t rather than a fully written ment sequence. The old word before live qui has a loop and dense cross-strokes that allow qui or et. The later soit/sont remains uncertain; au has a low rounded a and u before the separate dessus. In the savoir list the middle sigma’s small index is a dark upright mass favoring1, now locally qualified.
